Journal: Vaccine  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-25  |  DOI: 10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.129078  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 91, 129078
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

阿富汗麻疹风险:免疫系统中断后的省际免疫缺口与疫情潜在风险

Measles outbreak risk in Afghanistan: provincial immunity gaps and epidemic potential following immunisation system disruption
作者： Rajeev Ranjan, Palwasha Anwari, Abigail Colson, Robert van der Meer
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦于2021年政治过渡后阿富汗免疫系统中断所导致的麻疹 暴发风险。核心问题是:常规麻疹含疫苗(MCV1)覆盖是否足以防止暴发,以及还需要哪些额外措施才能实现群体免疫。
证据基础与方法
证据基础包括2022-23年阿富汗多重指标簇调查(MICS)数据,提供了各省份MCV1覆盖率的估计。综述采用确定性SEIR情景模拟和随机tau跳跃模拟来建模麻疹 传播潜在风险。使用R0=15和单剂疫苗效果(VE1)=0.93计算了覆盖率调整的传播潜在指数Rcov。研究还量化了调查不确定性、常规MCV1保护缺口上限以及在优先和随机目标策略下的补充免疫活动(SIA)需求。
共识与结论
综述得出结论:在中心假设下,仅依靠常规MCV1覆盖在数学上不足以达到群体免疫阈值。然而,高完整两剂系列覆盖率理论上仍有可能达到该阈值,但操作上具有挑战性。监测数据显示与Rcov存在正序地理一致性,表明覆盖率与暴发风险之间可能存在联系。
争议与缺口
综述指出,尚未利用家庭调查数据量化省际覆盖率衍生的麻疹 传播潜在风险。此外,监测比较是回顾性的,支持地理一致性而非外部模型验证。还缺乏关于免疫系统中断长期影响以及不同情境下SIA效果的数据。
科研与实践启示
综述强调需要持续的常规两剂接种、高覆盖率的SIA以及恢复免疫系统能力以减轻麻疹 暴发风险。同时建议进一步研究以量化各省份传播潜在风险的异质性,并评估不同目标策略下SIA的效果。

Measles outbreak risk in Afghanistan: provincial immunity gaps and epidemic potential following immunisation system disruption

Authors: Rajeev Ranjan, Palwasha Anwari, Abigail Colson, Robert van der Meer
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Following the 2021 political transition, Afghanistan's routine immunisation system experienced disruption. The 2022-23 Afghanistan Multiple Indicator Cluster Survey (MICS) estimated national first-dose measles-containing vaccine (MCV1) coverage at 51.2% (95% CI 49.1-53.2). Provincial heterogeneity in coverage-derived measles transmission potential has not been quantified using these household survey data. METHODS: Provincial MCV1 coverage among children aged 12-23 months was obtained from the 2022-23 MICS (unweighted n = 6177; weighted n = 6383). We calculated a coverage-adjusted transmission-potential index, Rcov = R0 × (1 - MCV1 × VE1), using R0 = 15 and one-dose vaccine effectiveness (VE1) = 0.93. Deterministic SEIR scenarios and complementary stochastic tau-leaping simulations were run using an 8-day mean latent period and 4-day infectious-compartment duration. Survey uncertainty, upper-bound routine-MCV1 protection gaps, and preferential- and random-targeting SIA requirements were quantified. RESULTS: Provincial MCV1 coverage ranged from 8.6% to 88.7%, and all 34 provinces had Rcov > 1 (range 2.63-13.80; U5-weighted aggregate 7.95). The theoretical one-dose coverage requirement was 100.36%, whereas the requirement for a complete two-dose series using empirical VE2 = 0.97 was 96.22%. The standardized 3.5-year upper-bound accumulation of children remaining unprotected by routine MCV1 was 3.02 million (conditional 95% interval 2.92-3.12 million). The province-summed SIA requirement was 4.06 million children (49.8% of U5) under idealized preferential targeting and 7.53 million (92.4%) under random targeting. Single-importation establishment probability ranged from 65.9% to 93.9% across nine illustrative provinces. WHO week-14 surveillance showed positive ordinal geographic concordance with Rcov (Kendall τb = 0.331; Spearman ρ = 0.430; both p = 0.011). CONCLUSIONS: Routine MCV1 alone is mathematically insufficient to reach the population-immunity threshold under the central assumptions. High complete two-dose-series coverage remains theoretically capable of reaching the threshold but is operationally demanding. Sustained routine two-dose delivery, high-reach SIAs, and restoration of immunisation-system capacity are likely to be required. The surveillance comparison is retrospective and supports geographic concordance rather than external model validation.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on measles outbreak risk in Afghanistan following the disruption of the immunisation system after the 2021 political transition. The central question is whether routine measles-containing vaccine (MCV1) coverage alone is sufficient to prevent outbreaks, and what additional measures are required to achieve population immunity.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base includes the 2022-23 Afghanistan Multiple Indicator Cluster Survey (MICS) data, which provided provincial MCV1 coverage estimates. The review used deterministic SEIR scenarios and stochastic tau-leaping simulations to model measles transmission potential. The coverage-adjusted transmission-potential index, Rcov, was calculated using R0 = 15 and one-dose vaccine effectiveness (VE1) = 0.93. The study also quantified survey uncertainty, upper-bound routine-MCV1 protection gaps, and the requirements for supplementary immunisation activities (SIAs) under preferential and random targeting.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that routine MCV1 coverage alone is mathematically insufficient to reach the population-immunity threshold under the central assumptions. However, high complete two-dose-series coverage remains theoretically capable of achieving the threshold, though it is operationally demanding. The surveillance data showed positive ordinal geographic concordance with Rcov, indicating a potential link between coverage and outbreak risk.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that provincial heterogeneity in coverage-derived measles transmission potential has not been quantified using the household survey data. Additionally, the surveillance comparison is retrospective and supports geographic concordance rather than external model validation. There is also a lack of data on the long-term impact of immunisation system disruption and the effectiveness of SIAs in different contexts.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for sustained routine two-dose delivery, high-reach SIAs, and the restoration of immunisation-system capacity to mitigate measles outbreak risk. It also suggests that further research is required to quantify provincial heterogeneity in transmission potential and to evaluate the effectiveness of SIAs under various targeting strategies.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

麻疹 前线防御:急诊护士和急救医疗服务人员的关键作用

Measles Frontline Defense: The Pivotal Role of Emergency Nurses and Emergency Medical Services Personnel
作者： Garrett D. Hall, Diana J. Felton, Nathan R. Tan, Sarah K. Kemble
摘要中文翻译简介:麻疹 于 2000 年在美国宣布消除。然而,2025 年,该国报告的病例数达到了 20 多年来的最高水平。此外,疾病控制与预防中心 2026 年初的数据表明病例数持续增加。急诊护士和急诊医疗技术人员经常作为最初的医疗保健专业人员来评估发烧和皮疹患者。及时识别和迅速隔离对于遏制 麻疹 传播 至关重要。这篇叙述性文献综述旨在为急诊护士和急诊医疗技术人员在 麻疹 暴发 期间的识别、隔离、记录、感染控制以及与公共卫生当局的协调方面提供实用的、基于证据的指导。方法:进行了综合当前疾病控制与预防中心和世界卫生组织指南的叙述性回顾。通过使用 麻疹、急诊科、院前、急诊护士、紧急医疗服务、分诊和感染控制等关键词在 PubMed 和 Google Scholar 中进行有针对性的搜索来确定同行评审文献,重点关注 2015 年至 2026 年的出版物。优先考虑提供与紧急医疗环境相关的实用指导或流行病学数据的研究。结果:麻疹 通过空气中的 传播 传播,并在感染者离开某个区域后可在空气中悬浮长达 2 小时。早期症状包括发烧、咳嗽、鼻炎(流鼻涕和鼻塞)和结膜炎,随后出现下降的斑丘疹(皮肤上有平坦区域和小肿块的红色皮疹),通常从面部开始并向下蔓延。适当的分诊评估、感染控制措施、电子健康记录中的记录以及与感染预防和公共卫生当局的及时沟通对于中断 传播 至关重要。讨论:麻疹 仍然是医疗保健环境中最具传染性的病毒性疾病之一。急诊护士和急救医疗技术人员往往是最先识别并阻止 麻疹 传播的人。应采用明确的分类和筛查步骤,应确认工作人员的免疫力,并与感染预防和公共卫生团队密切合作,以保护患者、家庭和医护人员。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
范围:麻疹病毒 (A1) 引起 麻疹 疾病,影响急诊护士和急诊医疗技术人员 (A4),核心问题是如何在紧急情况下识别、隔离、记录和控制 麻疹 传播 (A5、A6)。
证据基础与方法
证据基础:综合 CDC 和 WHO 指南的叙述性审查 (A7),在 PubMed 和 Google Scholar 中搜索 2015-2026 年有关紧急情况下 麻疹 的文献 (A8),并优先考虑实用指南和流行病学数据 (A9)。
共识与结论
共识:麻疹具有高度传染性且为空气传播(A10);早期症状包括发热、咳嗽、鼻炎、结膜炎和皮疹(A11);及时分诊、隔离、感染控制、记录、沟通、工作人员免疫力验证及公共卫生协作是中断传播的关键(A12、A13、A14、A15)。
争议与缺口
争议与缺口:在消除该疾病二十年后再次出现(A2、A3),以及关于最佳急诊分诊和监测方案的数据有限(A3、A15),表明标准化响应框架中的具体证据缺口。
科研与实践启示
启示:实施明确的分诊和筛查步骤,验证工作人员免疫力,强制执行感染控制措施,在电子健康记录中记录,并与感染预防和公共卫生当局保持及时沟通,以保护患者和工作人员(A6、A12、A15)。

Measles Frontline Defense: The Pivotal Role of Emergency Nurses and Emergency Medical Services Personnel

Authors: Garrett D. Hall, Diana J. Felton, Nathan R. Tan, Sarah K. Kemble
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: Measles was declared eliminated in the United States in 2000. However, in 2025, the country experienced the highest number of cases reported in more than 2 decades. Furthermore, early 2026 data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention indicate a continued increase in case counts. Emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians frequently serve as the initial health care professionals who assess patients with fever and rashes. Prompt identification and swift isolation are essential to curtail measles transmission. This narrative literature review aims to provide practical, evidence-based guidance for emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians on the recognition, isolation, documentation, infection control, and coordination with public health authorities during measles outbreaks. METHODS: A narrative review synthesizing current Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and World Health Organization guidance was conducted. Peer-reviewed literature was identified through targeted searches in PubMed and Google Scholar using keywords, including measles, emergency department, prehospital, emergency nurse, emergency medical services, triage, and infection control, focusing on publications from 2015 to 2026. Studies providing practical guidance or epidemiologic data relevant to emergency medical settings were prioritized. RESULTS: Measles spreads through airborne transmission and can remain suspended in the air for up to 2 hours after an infected individual leaves an area. Early symptoms include fever, cough, coryza (runny nose and nasal congestion), and conjunctivitis, followed by a descending maculopapular rash (red rash with both flat areas and small bumps on the skin), which typically begins on the face and spreads downward. Proper triage assessment, infection-control measures, documentation within electronic health records, and timely communication with infection prevention and public health authorities are essential to interrupt the transmission. DISCUSSION: Measles remains one of the most contagious viral illnesses encountered in health care settings. Emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians are often the first to recognize and halt the spread of measles. Clear triage and screening steps should be used, staff immunity should be confirmed, and close collaboration with infection prevention and public health teams is necessary to protect patients, families, and health care workers.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Scope: Measles virus (A1) causing measles disease, affecting emergency nurses and emergency medical technicians (A4) with the central question of how to recognize, isolate, document, and control measles transmission in emergency settings (A5, A6).
Evidence base and method
Evidence base: Narrative review synthesizing CDC and WHO guidance (A7), searching PubMed and Google Scholar for 2015‐2026 literature on measles in emergency settings (A8), and prioritizing practical guidance and epidemiologic data (A9).
Consensus and conclusions
Consensus: Measles is highly contagious and airborne (A10); early symptoms include fever, cough, coryza, conjunctivitis, and rash (A11); prompt triage, isolation, infection‐control, documentation, communication, staff immunity verification, and public‐health collaboration are essential to interrupt transmission (A12, A13, A14, A15).
Controversies and gaps
Controversies and gaps: The resurgence of measles after two decades of elimination (A2, A3) and limited data on optimal emergency triage and surveillance protocols (A3, A15) indicate concrete evidence gaps in standardized response frameworks.
Research and practice implications
Implications: Implement clear triage and screening steps, verify staff immunity, enforce infection‐control measures, document in electronic health records, and maintain timely communication with infection‐prevention and public‐health authorities to protect patients and staff (A6, A12, A15).

伊朗南部斑点热群立克次体病的罕见病例:诊断挑战与综述

A rare case of spotted fever group rickettsiosis in southern Iran: diagnostic challenges and review.
作者： Elham Barahimi, Abbas Poudat, Mehdi Hassani-Azad, Parivash Davoodian, Fatemeh Khajavi-Mayvan, MohammadHosein Sheybani-Arani, Mehdi Hassaniazad, MohammadHosein Sheybani‐Arani
摘要中文翻译背景:地中海斑点热(MSF)由康氏立克次体(Rickettsia conorii)引起,通过血红扇头蜱(Rhipicephalus sanguineus)传播,在在地中海地区流行。在伊朗等非流行国家,斑点热群(SFG)立克次体病罕有报道,且因与麻疹等常见疾病及登革热的临床重叠,诊断面临显著挑战。病例报告:一名来自伊朗南部霍尔木兹甘省的58岁男性患者,患有糖尿病和缺血性心脏病,出现两周高热、寒战、乏力、斑丘疹及严重肌痛。初始误诊为麻疹并排除登革热后,进一步询问病史显示其职业接触印度小沙鼠(Tatera indica)。体格检查显示体温39.5 °C、四肢及躯干皮疹,以及小腿焦痂。关键实验室检查显示AST(118 U/L)、ALT(106 U/L)及CRP(33.98 mg/L)升高。经验性给予多西环素(100 mg,每日两次)。间接免疫荧光法(IFA)IgM滴度为1:768(高滴度),后续恢复期血清样本显示IgG血清转换。体温于48小时内恢复正常;患者症状及实验室指标改善后出院。结论:本病例展示了斑点热群立克次体病的诊断挑战,最符合伊朗非流行地区的疑似地中海斑点热。强调详细暴露史及经验性多西环素治疗在疑似病例中的价值。亟需提高认识、改善诊断可及性及加强监测,以更好地识别伊朗(尤其是霍尔木兹甘省)的此类感染。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦伊朗南部斑点热群立克次体病的罕见病例,强调诊断挑战并重视麻疹等鉴别诊断及登革热的重要性。核心问题是斑点热群立克次体病(特别是地中海斑点热)是否可在伊朗等非流行地区被准确诊断。
证据基础与方法
本综述基于伊朗南部的一项病例报告。证据包括临床表现、实验室检查结果及血清学检测。方法涉及经验性多西环素治疗及间接免疫荧光法(IFA)IgM和IgG血清学确认。本综述未进行荟萃分析或汇总估计。
共识与结论
病例报告表明,斑点热群立克次体病(最可能为地中海斑点热)在伊朗等非流行地区因临床表现与麻疹等疾病高度相似,诊断面临显著挑战。诊断通过血清学检测及对经验性多西环素治疗的临床反应得到确认。
争议与缺口
综述指出,斑点热群立克次体病在伊朗等非流行国家罕有报道,可能反映了漏报或诊断能力有限。该地区缺乏此类感染的流行病学广泛数据,且病例报告未提供疾病传播动力学或宿主相关信息。
科研与实践启示
本病例提示伊朗(特别是南部省份如霍尔木兹甘省)的医护人员需提高对斑点热群立克次体病的认识。同时强调改善诊断工具可及性及加强监测的重要性,以更好地识别和管理此类感染。

A rare case of spotted fever group rickettsiosis in southern Iran: diagnostic challenges and review.

Authors: Elham Barahimi, Abbas Poudat, Mehdi Hassani-Azad, Parivash Davoodian, Fatemeh Khajavi-Mayvan, MohammadHosein Sheybani-Arani, Mehdi Hassaniazad, MohammadHosein Sheybani‐Arani
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Mediterranean spotted fever (MSF), caused by Rickettsia conorii and transmitted by the brown dog tick (Rhipicephalus sanguineus), is endemic in Mediterranean regions. In non-endemic countries such as Iran, spotted fever group (SFG) rickettsioses are rarely reported and present significant diagnostic challenges due to overlap with common illnesses like measles and dengue fever. CASE PRESENTATION: A 58-year-old man from Hormozgan Province, southern Iran, with diabetes and ischemic heart disease, presented with two weeks of high fever, chills, weakness, maculopapular rash, and severe myalgia. Initially misdiagnosed as measles and tested negative for dengue, further history revealed occupational contact with Tatera indica rodents. Examination revealed fever (39.5 °C), rash on the extremities and trunk, and a calf eschar. Key labs included elevated AST (118 U/L), ALT (106 U/L) and CRP (33.98 mg/L). Empirical doxycycline (100 mg twice daily) was started. IFA IgM was positive at a high titer (1:768), with a subsequent convalescent sample showing IgG seroconversion. Fever resolved within 48 h; he was discharged after five days with improving symptoms and labs. CONCLUSION: This case illustrates the diagnostic challenges of SFG rickettsioses, most consistent with probable MSF, in a non-endemic region of Iran. It potentially emphasises the value of detailed exposure history and prompt empirical doxycycline in suspected cases. Greater awareness, improved access to diagnostics, and enhanced surveillance are needed to better recognise these infections in Iran, especially in Hormozgan province.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on a rare case of spotted fever group rickettsiosis in southern Iran, highlighting diagnostic challenges and the importance of considering differential diagnoses such as measles and dengue fever. The central question is whether SFG rickettsioses, particularly Mediterranean spotted fever (MSF), can be accurately diagnosed in non-endemic regions like Iran.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a single case report from southern Iran. The evidence includes clinical presentation, laboratory findings, and serological testing. The methods involve empirical treatment with doxycycline and serological confirmation using IFA IgM and IgG tests. The review does not involve a meta-analysis or pooled estimates.
Consensus and conclusions
The case report suggests that SFG rickettsioses, most likely MSF, present significant diagnostic challenges in non-endemic regions of Iran due to their clinical similarity with other diseases such as measles and dengue fever. The diagnosis was confirmed through serological testing and clinical response to treatment.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that SFG rickettsioses are rarely reported in non-endemic countries like Iran, which may indicate underreporting or limited diagnostic capabilities. There is a lack of broader epidemiological data on these infections in the region, and the case report does not provide information on the prevalence or transmission dynamics of the disease.
Research and practice implications
The case underscores the need for greater awareness of SFG rickettsioses among healthcare providers in Iran, particularly in southern provinces like Hormozgan. It also highlights the importance of improved access to diagnostic tools and enhanced surveillance to better identify and manage these infections.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

巴西一家参考中心随访的慢性肝病患者疫苗接种状况

VACCINATION STATUS OF PATIENTS WITH CHRONIC LIVER DISEASE FOLLOWED UP AT A REFERENCE CENTER IN BRAZIL
作者： Maria Elizabete Calore Neiva, Maria Lucia Gomes Ferraz, R. Gama, Antonio Eduardo Benedito Silva
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述关注巴西一家参考中心慢性肝病(CLD)患者的疫苗接种状况。核心问题是:考虑到其免疫功能障碍,这些患者是否已充分接种可预防传染病的疫苗?
证据基础与方法
本综述为一项横断面观察性研究,在圣保罗大学医学院附属医院圣保罗医院肝病门诊进行。研究采用不同病因慢性肝病成人患者的方便样本。参与者接受标准化访谈、疫苗接种记录查阅及甲型肝炎和乙型肝炎血清学检测。
共识与结论
在104名患者中,慢性丙型肝炎是最常见的慢性肝病病因(63.5%),其次为乙型肝炎(10.6%)。46.1%的患者存在进展期肝纤维化。仅45.1%显示乙型肝炎免疫血清学证据,75%的患者检测到抗-HAV IgG阳性,但仅14.4%记录有甲型肝炎疫苗接种史。其他疫苗的接种覆盖率亦低于预期,仅23%的患者记录有麻疹-腮腺炎-风疹疫苗接种史。
争议与缺口
本综述凸显了慢性肝病患者在多项推荐疫苗接种覆盖率方面存在差距,即便在专科转诊中心亦是如此。研究未提供低疫苗接种率的原因或此类人群疫苗接种策略有效性的信息。
科研与实践启示
研究结果强调了系统评估慢性肝病患者免疫状况及加强其疫苗接种策略的必要性。这包括改善接种记录并确保患者在免疫功能障碍情况下仍能接种推荐疫苗。

VACCINATION STATUS OF PATIENTS WITH CHRONIC LIVER DISEASE FOLLOWED UP AT A REFERENCE CENTER IN BRAZIL

Authors: Maria Elizabete Calore Neiva, Maria Lucia Gomes Ferraz, R. Gama, Antonio Eduardo Benedito Silva
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background: Patients with chronic liver disease (CLD) often experience varying degrees of immune dysfunction, making them vulnerable to multiple preventable infectious diseases through appropriate vaccination. Objective: To evaluate vaccination status and vaccine coverage among patients with chronic liver disease (CLD) receiving care at a tertiary referral center. Methods: This cross-sectional observational study was conducted at the Hepatology Outpatient Clinic of Hospital São Paulo, Escola Paulista de Medicina, Federal University of São Paulo (UNIFESP), São Paulo, Brazil. A convenience sample of adult patients with different etiologies of CLD was prospectively included. Participants underwent a standardized interview, review of vaccination records, and serological testing for hepatitis A and hepatitis B. Results: Among 104 patients (61.5% women, median age 51 years), chronic hepatitis C (63.5%) and hepatitis B (10.6%) were the most common causes of CLD. Advanced liver fibrosis was identified in 46.1% of the patients. Vaccination records were available for 82.7% of participants, but only 45.1% showed serological evidence of hepatitis B immunization (anti-HBs positive). Positive anti-HAV IgG serology was detected in 75% of patients, whereas only 14.4% had documented hepatitis A vaccination. Vaccination coverage for other vaccines was as follows: meningococcal conjugate vaccine (8.7%), pneumococcal vaccine PPSV23 (25%), influenza vaccine (53.8%), tetanus-diphtheria vaccine (60.6%), measles-mumps-rubella vaccine (23%), yellow fever vaccine (28.8%), and COVID-19 vaccination coverage was 92.3%. Conclusion: Vaccination coverage among patients with chronic liver disease remained lower than expected for several recommended vaccines, even in a specialized referral center. These findings highlight the importance of systematic assessment of immunization status and reinforcement of vaccination strategies in patients with chronic liver disease.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the vaccination status of patients with chronic liver disease (CLD) at a reference center in Brazil. The central question is whether these patients are adequately vaccinated against preventable infectious diseases, given their immune dysfunction.
Evidence base and method
The review is a cross-sectional observational study conducted at the Hepatology Outpatient Clinic of Hospital São Paulo, Escola Paulista de Medicina, Federal University of São Paulo (UNIFESP), São Paulo, Brazil. It uses a convenience sample of adult patients with different etiologies of CLD. Participants underwent a standardized interview, review of vaccination records, and serological testing for hepatitis A and hepatitis B.
Consensus and conclusions
Among 104 patients, chronic hepatitis C was the most common cause of CLD (63.5%), followed by hepatitis B (10.6%). Advanced liver fibrosis was identified in 46.1% of the patients. Only 45.1% showed serological evidence of hepatitis B immunization, and 75% had positive anti-HAV IgG serology, though only 14.4% had documented hepatitis A vaccination. Vaccination coverage for other vaccines was also lower than expected, with only 23% having measles-mumps-rubella vaccine documentation.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights a gap in vaccination coverage for several recommended vaccines among patients with CLD, even in a specialized referral center. The study does not provide information on the reasons for low vaccination rates or the effectiveness of vaccination strategies in this population.
Research and practice implications
The findings emphasize the need for systematic assessment of immunization status and reinforcement of vaccination strategies in patients with chronic liver disease. This includes improving documentation and ensuring that patients receive recommended vaccines despite their immune dysfunction.

麻疹 与水痘保护 - 病例激增,但希望再现!

Measles and varicella protection - surge in cases, but now a surge in hope!
作者： Robert J. Rolfe, Cameron R Wolfe, Robert J Rolfe, Rolfe RJ, Wolfe CR., Cameron R. Wolfe, Robert Rolfe

Measles and varicella protection - surge in cases, but now a surge in hope!

马里兰州卫生部在马里兰州三个县宣布麻疹 暴发 - Maryland.gov

Pages - Maryland Department of Health declares measles outbreak in three Maryland counties - Maryland.gov
新闻简报马里兰州卫生部已在三个县宣布麻疹 暴发:塔尔博特县、华盛顿县和威科米科县。该官方声明发布于2026年8月24日。此次暴发已确认,但所提供的证据中未报告具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。卫生部门正在开展调查,但所提供的证据中未包含检测、隔离、密切接触者追踪或预防措施的详细信息。此次暴发是针对麻疹这一高度传染性病毒性疾病的更广泛公共卫生应对措施的一部分。该声明强调需在社区内加强警惕并采取潜在干预措施以控制病毒传播。

时间
您可能正在通过服务器上的安全浏览器访问此网站。
地点与对象
马里兰州,具体为三个县:塔尔博特县、华盛顿县和威科米科县。
事件
马里兰州卫生部在马里兰州三个县宣布麻疹 暴发。此为卫生部门的官方声明。
规模、影响与风险

应对、状态与不确定性
此次暴发正由马里兰州卫生部进行调查。所提供的证据中未包含检测、隔离、密切接触者追踪或预防措施的详细信息。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Pages - Maryland Department of Health declares measles outbreak in three Maryland counties - Maryland.gov

News Brief
The Maryland Department of Health has declared a measles outbreak in three counties: Talbot, Washington, and Wicomico. This official statement was published on August 24, 2026. The outbreak is confirmed, but no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers are reported in the evidence. The health department is conducting an investigation, but details on testing, isolation, contact tracing, or prevention measures are not included. The outbreak is part of the broader public health response to measles, which is a highly contagious viral disease. The declaration highlights the need for increased vigilance and potential interventions to control the spread of the virus within these communities.

News Brief
The CDC has reported that measles cases in the United States have exceeded 2,000 for the second year in a row, as noted in a news article published on August 27, 2026. The article highlights the ongoing public health concern related to the measles virus, which is a primary target under the topic contract. The evidence provided does not specify the exact number of deaths or the risk level associated with the outbreak. However, it indicates that the CDC is aware of the situation and is monitoring it. The article also includes various other news items, such as updates on the Lindsay Clancy trial, Nepal flooding, and Dolly Parton's legacy, which are not directly related to the measles outbreak. The content status is full, meaning the entire original article body was available for analysis. The source is considered reputable media, and the confidence level is moderate due to the lack of detailed information on the outbreak's specifics.

宾夕法尼亚州两名未接种疫苗者死于麻疹——<卫报>

Two unvaccinated people die of measles in Pennsylvania - The Guardian
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚州两名未接种疫苗者死于麻疹,这是该州35年来首次出现此类死亡病例。死亡病例于2026年8月24日(周二)公布,仅在马里兰州宣布在三个县暴发麻疹疫情的次日。该麻疹病毒(属于麻疹病毒属)具有高度传染性且可通过MMR疫苗预防,近期病例数上升,宾夕法尼亚州全州28个县报告了近400例病例。大多数病例发生在18至49岁的成年人中,约20%的未接种疫苗的麻疹患者需住院治疗。宾夕法尼亚州官员未提供进一步详情,但强调了疫苗接种的重要性。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)指出,麻疹疫苗豁免申请激增,这可能与近期暴发有关。麻疹病毒可引发严重健康问题,包括肺炎和脑肿胀。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Published on 2026-08-26. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the deaths were announced on Tuesday, August 24, 2026. The outbreak in Maryland was declared on Monday, August 23, 2026.
地点与对象
宾夕法尼亚州,具体为兰开斯特县。受影响人群为未接种疫苗者,大多数病例报告在18至49岁成年人中。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州两名未接种疫苗者死于麻疹。这是该州35年来首次出现麻疹相关死亡病例。此次死亡发生在马里兰州宣布在三个县暴发麻疹疫情的次日。
规模、影响与风险
宾夕法尼亚州确诊31例病例,全州28个县报告近400例病例。麻疹病毒具有高度传染性,可导致严重健康并发症,包括肺炎和脑肿胀。约20%的未接种疫苗的麻疹患者需住院治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
宾夕法尼亚州官员未发布进一步信息。该州卫生部长Debra Bogen博士强调了疫苗接种的重要性及MMR疫苗的安全性。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)报告麻疹疫苗豁免申请激增,这可能加剧麻疹传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Two unvaccinated people die of measles in Pennsylvania - The Guardian

News Brief
Two unvaccinated individuals have died of measles in Pennsylvania, marking the first such deaths in the state in 35 years. The deaths were announced on Tuesday, August 24, 2026, just one day after Maryland declared a measles outbreak in three counties. The measles virus, which is highly contagious and preventable through the MMR vaccine, has seen a rise in cases, with nearly 400 reported across 28 counties in Pennsylvania. Most cases are in adults aged 18 to 49, and about 20% of unvaccinated people who contract measles are hospitalized. Pennsylvania officials have not provided further details, but they have stressed the importance of vaccination. The CDC noted a significant increase in vaccine exemption requests, which may be linked to the recent outbreaks. The measles virus belongs to the Morbillivirus family and is known to cause severe health issues, including pneumonia and brain swelling.
Original source text (English)

Photograph: BSIP/Universal Images Group/Getty Images View image in fullscreen The measles virus, paramyxoviridae from the Morbillivirus family, transmission microscopy view. Photograph: BSIP/Universal Images Group/Getty Images Two unvaccinated people die of measles in Pennsylvania Deaths from disease are first in state in 35 years and just one day after Maryland declared outbreak in three counties Two unvaccinated individuals have died of measles in Pennsylvania, state health officials announced on Tuesday. The deaths are the first in the state in 35 years and have been announced just one day after Maryland declared a measles outbreak in three counties. Measles is one of the most contagious diseases known to medicine, and is preventable with a highly effective vaccine. “My deepest sympathies are with the loved ones who are facing this unimaginable loss,” said the Pennsylvania secretary of health, Dr Debra Bogen, in a statement. “Because measles was largely eliminated in the Commonwealth for more than three decades, people are not familiar with this disease and don’t fully understand the potential severity of the illness.” Measles kills one to three people for every 1,000 it infects, and can lead to short- and long-term health problems, including pneumonia and brain swelling. About 20% of unvaccinated people who contract measles will be hospitalized. “As a physician, I want to make sure that people understand that the [measles, mumps and rubella] vaccine is safe and provides the best protection we have against measles,” said Bogen, referring to what clinicians call the MMR vaccine. On Monday, Maryland declared a measles outbreak in St Mary’s, Carroll and Cecil counties. There are 31 confirmed cases in the state. New York urged residents to get vaccinated on Monday, citing an uptick in cases in rural parts of the state around the capital, central and western regions. “The overall risk remains low if you are fully vaccinated,” said Maryland’s health department secretary, Dr Meena Seshamani, in a statement. “We ask that Marylanders join us in our effort to help keep our youngest safe and protect those who are most at risk. Checking your vaccination status and getting vaccinated if you are not up-to-date is the simplest and most effective way to do that.” The news comes just one week after the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) released data showing the largest ever one-year increase in requests for vaccine exemptions – the process by which parents can request not to have their child vaccinated and still send them to school. The same data showed a long, slow decline in vaccination rates, particularly for measles. The news also comes after Donald Trump and his health secretary, Robert F Kennedy Jr, held a press conference in which they linked vaccines to autism, a decades-old claim from the anti-vaccine movement that has been repeatedly debunked by scientific studies. Pennsylvania state officials said both individuals who died from measles were residents of Lancaster county. No further information was released. The state has counted nearly 400 cases across 28 counties in Pennsylvania, nearly half of which are in southern Lancaster county. Most of the cases reported to state health authorities are in adults between 18 and 49. Official tallies are typically an undercount of actual cases. 达拉斯县卫生官员确认一例麻疹病例,警告北德州其他民众可能暴露 - KERA新闻

Dallas County health officials confirm measles case, warn other North Texans of possible exposure - KERA News
新闻简报达拉斯县卫生官员于2026年8月26日确认一例麻疹病例,并就北德州三处地点可能存在的暴露风险发出警告:Turrentine Jackson Morrow教堂、Gloria’s餐厅及The Revel露台烧烤。该患者在2026年8月25日特定时间窗口具有传染性。卫生官员强调接种MMR 疫苗的重要性,并指出未接种疫苗者及高风险人群(如婴幼儿、无免疫力孕妇及免疫系统严重受损者)罹患严重并发症的风险增加。官方建议暴露者检查自身疫苗接种状态,并在21天内监测症状。出现症状者应避免乘坐公共交通工具,并在就医前告知医护人员潜在暴露史。该病例是全国麻疹病例激增的一部分,2026年全美报告病例已超过2,750例,引发对疫苗接种率下降及潜在暴发的担忧。
查看新闻五要素
时间
达拉斯县卫生官员于2026年8月26日确认该麻疹病例。该患者于2026年8月25日在北德州三处地点的特定时间窗口具有传染性。
地点与对象
达拉斯县、北德州,涉及地点包括Turrentine Jackson Morrow教堂、Gloria’s餐厅及The Revel露台烧烤。受影响人群包括未接种疫苗者及高风险人群,如婴幼儿、无免疫力孕妇及免疫系统严重受损者。
事件
达拉斯县卫生官员于2026年8月26日确认一例麻疹病例,并就北德州三处地点可能存在的暴露风险发出警告。该病例由KERA新闻于2026年8月26日报道。
规模、影响与风险
该麻疹病例是全国范围内更大规模疫情的一部分,2026年全美报告病例已超过2,750例。未接种疫苗者及高风险人群面临更高风险,若暴露可能引发严重并发症。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dallas County health officials confirm measles case, warn other North Texans of possible exposure - KERA News

News Brief
Dallas County health officials confirmed a measles case on August 26, 2026, and issued warnings about possible exposure at three locations in North Texas: Turrentine Jackson Morrow Chapel, Gloria’s Restaurant, and The Revel Patio Grill. The individual was infectious during specific time windows on August 25, 2026. Health officials emphasized the importance of the MMR vaccine, noting that unvaccinated individuals and high-risk groups, such as infants, pregnant people without immunity, and those with weakened immune systems, are at increased risk for serious complications. They advised exposed individuals to check their vaccination status and monitor for symptoms for 21 days. Those showing symptoms should avoid public transportation and inform healthcare providers before visiting. The case is part of a national rise in measles cases, with over 2,750 reported in 2026, raising concerns about declining vaccination rates and potential outbreaks.
Original source text (English)

The highly contagious virus can remain infectious in the air for up to two hours after an infected person leaves the area. Dallas County health officials are warning of possible measles exposure after a resident tested positive for the virus. Dallas County Health and Human Services said Tuesday the individual visited three North Texas locations while infectious on Aug. 14 and Aug. 15. The highly contagious virus can remain infectious in the air for up to two hours after an infected person leaves the area. Dallas County identified the following windows of risk: Turrentine Jackson Morrow Chapel, 2525 Central Expressway North, Allen, 75013 Full window of risk: Friday, Aug. 14 from 10 a.m. to 2 p.m. Gloria’s Restaurant, 152 Fountain Circle, Fairview, 75069 Full window of risk: Friday, Aug. 14 from 12:30 p.m. to 5 p.m. The Revel Patio Grill, 9305 Preston Rd, Frisco, 75033 Full window of risk: Saturday, Aug. 15 from 7:45 p.m. to midnight Advice from health officials “If you were at one of these locations during the identified time period, check your vaccination status and watch closely for symptoms,” Dr. Philip Huang, director of the Dallas County health department, said in a statement. “Most importantly, make sure you and your family are up to date on the MMR vaccine.” Dallas County Health and Human Services said people who are concerned about exposure should check if they’ve gotten the MMR vaccine. The department said people who have documentation of vaccination, “laboratory evidence of immunity” or confirmation of previous measles infection are considered protected against the virus. It also said people born before 1957 are also generally presumed to have immunity. People who are unvaccinated and were present at any of the three locations during a window of potential risk should monitor for symptoms for 21 days after exposure. Symptoms can include high fever, cough, runny nose, a progressive rash that begins on the face and spreads down the body and red, watery eyes. Infants, pregnant people “without evidence of immunity” and people with severely weakened immune systems are at an increased risk for serious complications from a measles infection. County health officials said anyone in higher risk groups who may have been exposed to the virus should contact a healthcare provider for guidance. The health department said people who develop symptoms of measles should stay home and avoid contact with others. It also urges people to call the healthcare provider or emergency department before arriving to tell them about the potential exposure to measles. When traveling to healthcare facilities, the county said people who are showing symptoms should avoid public transportation, taxis and ride-share services. Rising concerns about measles The number of measles cases is higher than it’s been in 35 years, with more than 2,750 cases reported across the country – already surpassing last year’s total of about 2,300, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. North Texas health officials said they’re concerned about declining vaccination rates, especially as students head back to school. They said falling rates could lead to even more outbreaks, like the historic 2025 measles outbreak in West Texas – which lasted for months and resulted in more than 750 cases across 37 counties. “Measles is one of the most contagious diseases we know, but it is also highly preventable,” said Huang. “Vaccination remains our best protection against measles and the best way to prevent further spread in our community.” Abigail Ruhman is KERA’s health reporter. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

麻疹 - 不列颠百科全书

measles - Britannica
新闻简报The article from Britannica provides general information about measles, a highly contagious viral disease. It outlines the symptoms, including fever, cough, and a characteristic rash, and notes that measles can lead to severe complications such as pneumonia, encephalitis, and even death, especially in unvaccinated individuals. The resurgence of measles is attributed to declining vaccination rates, which have fallen below the 95 percent threshold needed for herd immunity. The MMR vaccine is highlighted as the primary prevention method, with an effectiveness rate of about 97 percent after two doses. However, breakthrough infections are possible, and vaccinated individuals who contract measles typically experience milder symptoms. The article does not report on specific outbreaks, cases, or containment efforts, focusing instead on general knowledge and historical context.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发布于2026-08-25,证据中未明确提及事件发生日期。
地点与对象
该证据未指明麻疹的具体发生地点或受影响人群。
事件
该文章讨论麻疹作为一种高度传染性病毒性疾病,其症状、流行病学特征及通过疫苗接种进行预防。文章还提及因麻疹疫苗接种率下降导致的麻疹再次流行,以及肺炎、脑炎和死亡等潜在并发症,尤其是在未接种疫苗的个体中。
规模、影响与风险
Measles is described as a disease that spreads easily in under-vaccinated communities. It can lead to severe complications, including pneumonia, encephalitis, and death, particularly in unvaccinated people. The MMR vaccine is noted to be about 97 percent effective after two doses, but breakthrough infections are possible.
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章提供关于麻疹的通用信息、其预防措施及流行病学特征,未报告具体调查、检测或防控措施。信息基于历史与通用知识,而非当前病例数据或具体应对措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

measles - Britannica

News Brief
The article from Britannica provides general information about measles, a highly contagious viral disease. It outlines the symptoms, including fever, cough, and a characteristic rash, and notes that measles can lead to severe complications such as pneumonia, encephalitis, and even death, especially in unvaccinated individuals. The resurgence of measles is attributed to declining vaccination rates, which have fallen below the 95 percent threshold needed for herd immunity. The MMR vaccine is highlighted as the primary prevention method, with an effectiveness rate of about 97 percent after two doses. However, breakthrough infections are possible, and vaccinated individuals who contract measles typically experience milder symptoms. The article does not report on specific outbreaks, cases, or containment efforts, focusing instead on general knowledge and historical context.

Britannica Editors Last updated Aug. 25, 2026 •History Britannica AI Ask Anything Quick Summary Table of Contents Table of Contents Quick Summary Ask Anything Top Questions What is measles? Measles is a highly contagious viral disease, a hallmark symptom of which is a blotchy rash. It is most common in children and can be prevented through vaccination. What happens if you get measles? If you get measles, you are likely to develop a high fever, cough, and runny nose and have red, watery eyes. These symptoms are followed by a characteristic rash that spreads from the face downward. Symptoms appear 7–14 days after exposure and can lead to severe complications, including pneumonia, encephalitis (brain inflammation), diarrhea, or death, particularly in people who are unvaccinated. Can you get measles if you had a vaccine? It is possible, though rare, to get measles even after being vaccinated. Such breakthrough infections occur because the MMR vaccine, which protects against measles, is about 97 percent effective after two doses. However, vaccinated individuals who become infected usually experience a milder illness and are less likely to spread it to others, providing crucial protection for vulnerable populations, including the very young and the elderly. Why is measles coming back? Measles is resurging primarily due to declining vaccination rates, which have dropped below the 95 percent threshold required for herd immunity. Increased vaccine hesitancy and interruptions in routine immunization schedules, particularly during the COVID-19 pandemic, have been major contributing factors to this decline. Because the measles virus is highly contagious, it spreads easily and quickly through under-vaccinated communities. What is the recommended treatment for measles? No drug is effective against measles. The only treatment required is control of fever, rest in bed, protection of the eyes, care of the bowels, and sometimes steam inhalations to relieve irritation of the bronchial tree. When no complications occur, the illness lasts 10 days. What are complications of measles? Measles complications are frequent and include superimposed bacterial ear infection, pneumonia, and lung infection. The virus can also cause hepatitis, appendicitis, and gangrene of the extremities. Encephalitis is a rare occurrence. Deaths attributed to measles usually result from secondary bronchopneumonia caused by bacterial organisms entering the inflamed bronchial tree. Can measles affect adults? Measles is most common in children but may appear in older persons who escaped it earlier in life. Immunity to measles following an attack is usually lifelong. Is there a vaccine for measles? Measles vaccines are live vaccines that work against measles alone or in combination with other agents, specifically with rubella (MR), mumps and rubella (MMR), or mumps, rubella, and varicella (MMRV). The vaccines are typically given in two doses, the first before 15 months of age and the second dose later; timing of the dosages varies by country or region. News • Pennsylvania reports first 2 measles deaths in the US this year, both people unvaccinated • Aug. 25, 2026, 4:35 PM ET (AP) ...(Show more) Correction: Trump-Vaccines story • Aug. 11, 2026, 10:09 AM ET (AP) Show less measles, contagious viral disease marked by fever, cough, conjunctivitis, and a characteristic rash. Measles is most common in children but may appear in older persons who escaped it earlier in life. Infants are immune up to four or five months of age if the mother has had the disease. Immunity to measles following an attack is usually lifelong. The disease can be prevented through immunization with the MMR vaccine. Learn more in this list of vaccine-preventable diseases. Transmission and symptoms MeaslesGiant cell in a patient with measles.(more) Measles is so highly communicable that the slightest contact with an active case may infect a susceptible person. After an incubation period of about 10 days, the patient develops fever, redness and watering of the eyes, profuse nasal discharge, and congestion of the mucous membranes of the nose and throat—symptoms often mistaken for those of a severe cold. This period of invasion lasts for 48 to 96 hours. The fever increases with appearance of a blotchy rash, and the temperature may rise as high as 40 °C (about 105 °F) when the rash reaches its maximum. Twenty-four to 36 hours before the rash develops, there appear in the mucous membranes of the mouth typical maculae, called Koplik spots—bluish white specks surrounded by bright red areas about 1/32 inch (0.75 mm) in diameter. After a day or two the rash becomes a deeper red and gradually fades, the temperature drops rapidly, and the catarrhal symptoms disappear. Britannica Quiz A Visit with the Word Doctor: Medical Vocabulary Quiz Treatment and complications No drug is effective against measles. The only treatment required is control of fever, rest in bed, protection of the eyes, care of the bowels, and sometimes steam inhalations to relieve irritation of the bronchial tree. When no complications occur, the illness lasts 10 days. Although uncomplicated measles is seldom fatal, infection with the virus has been shown to induce a form of “immune amnesia,” whereby measles virus eliminates as many as half of the antibodies generated against other infectious agents to which an individual was exposed previously. Thus, persons who survive measles infection may become vulnerable once again to a range of other diseases, such as chickenpox and polio. By contrast, individuals vaccinated against measles do not experience a loss of immunity to other infectious agents. Deaths attributed to measles usually result from secondary bronchopneumonia caused by bacterial organisms entering the inflamed bronchial tree. Complications of measles are frequent and include a superimposed bacterial ear infection or pneumonia or a primary measles lung infection. Encephalitis is a rare occurrence. Measles virus can invade various organ systems and cause hepatitis, appendicitis, and gangrene of the extremities. A large percentage of cases of severe measles are associated with inadequate intake of vitamin A, and there is evidence that treatment with vitamin A may reduce measles complications. On very rare occasions, persistent infection with a mutant measles virus can cause a degenerative central nervous system disease called subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), in which there is a gradual onset of progressive behavioral and intellectual deterioration. Motor incoordination and impairment of speech and sight subsequently develop. The final stages of stupor, dementia, blindness, and death occur within six to nine months. There is no treatment for SSPE. Measles vaccine and eradication efforts How We Develop New VaccinesWorried about diphtheria? No? Thank vaccines.(more) See all videos for this article Mortality caused by measles declined steadily in the 20th century as the health of children and infants improved and effective treatment of complications became possible through the use of sulfonamide and antibiotic drugs. The widespread use of measles vaccine, beginning in the late 1960s, raised hopes for the eventual eradication of the disease. In the following decades, however, measles remained a leading cause of childhood mortality worldwide, primarily because attack rates remained high in less-developed countries, where factors such as malnutrition and weak public health infrastructure challenged infant health and the establishment of immunization programs. In the early 21st century, campaigns were initiated to increase vaccination, particularly in less-developed countries. International efforts led to a significant reduction in measles cases and deaths, bringing global elimination of the disease within reach. At the same time, supporting this progress, many wealthier countries, including the United States and some countries in the European Union, had successfully eradicated measles. Explore Britannica Premium! The trusted destination for professionals, college students, and lifelong learners. SUBSCRIBE In the second decade of the 21st century, however, large measles outbreaks continued to occur in countries with low vaccination rates. In late 2019 in Samoa, for instance, a severe outbreak of measles resulted in school closures and government shutdown, followed by a mass vaccination campaign to prevent further spread of the disease. Measles also reemerged in several countries where it previously had been eradicated, a trend attributed to alarming declines in vaccination coverage. Of particular concern was the return of measles in developed countries such as the United States, where the disease had been eliminated by 2000, and the United Kingdom. In these countries, measles reemerged in the form of small outbreaks that were typically concentrated in areas with relatively high proportions of unvaccinated individuals. In early 2025, the death of a child amid a measles outbreak in Texas marked the country’s first death caused by measles in more than a decade. Coincident with the return of measles in more developed countries, however, was a massive surge in the disease in countries with traditionally low vaccination coverage, resulting in large increases in measles cases worldwide. Global incidence was notably high in 2019, with more than 364,800 cases reported between June and July alone that year—far exceeding the number of cases reported worldwide over that time frame for any year since 2006, according to officials with the World Health Organization (WHO). The largest increases occurred in countries in Africa, Europe, and the Western Pacific. By early 2020 the Democratic Republic of the Congo was in the midst of a severe measles epidemic, with more than 6,000 deaths according to WHO. WHO and health officials in regions affected by measles outbreaks increased efforts to bolster vaccination rates to stop the disease from spreading further. Find out from Dr. Tina Tan of Northwestern University why adult vaccination against various types of diseases is importantLearn about the importance of adult vaccination against measles and other diseases.(more) See all videos for this article Measles vaccines are live vaccines that work against measles alone or in combination against other agents, specifically with rubella (MR), mumps and rubella (MMR), or mumps, rubella, and varicella (MMRV). The vaccines typically are given in two doses. In the United States, for example, the first dose is given at 12 to 15 months of age, and the second dose is recommended at four to six years. In other countries, the vaccine is given first at nine months and the second dose later. The second dose of MMR must be given at least four weeks after the first dose; in adults whose vaccination status is uncertain, the two doses typically are given four weeks apart. The youngest age at which the vaccines can be given is six months, though revaccination (with two doses) is needed later. See MMR vaccine. Similar illnesses Measles must be differentiated from other disorders accompanied by an eruption. In roseola infantum, a disease seen in babies, a measleslike rash appears after the child has had a high temperature for two or three days, but there is no fever at the time of the rash. German measles (rubella) can be superficially differentiated from measles by the shorter course of the disease and mildness of the symptoms. Sometimes the rashes of scarlet fever, serum reactions, and other conditions may, on certain parts of the body, look like measles. Drugs that may produce rashes similar to measles are phenobarbital, diphenylhydantoin, the sulfonamides, phenolphthalein, and penicillin. Britannica Editors Last updated Aug. 28, 2026 •History Britannica AI Ask Anything Table of Contents Table of Contents Ask Anything Top Questions What is a vaccine? A vaccine is a suspension of weakened, killed, or fragmented microorganisms or toxins or other biological preparation, such as those consisting of antibodies, lymphocytes, or mRNA, that is administered primarily to prevent disease. How are vaccines made? A vaccine is produced by first generating the antigen—the component that stimulates the immune system to respond. This antigen can take several forms, including an inactivated virus or bacterium, a purified subunit of the pathogen, or a recombinant protein engineered from it. Once the antigen is produced, it is carefully isolated and purified. Additional ingredients are then incorporated to boost its effectiveness and maintain stability during storage. Finally, the vaccine is manufactured on a large scale and packaged for widespread distribution. What is a vaccine delivery system? A vaccine delivery system refers to the method used to package and administer the immune-stimulating component of a vaccine so that it reaches the appropriate tissue in the body. Common examples include liposomes, emulsions, and microparticles. How do vaccines work? A vaccine works by mimicking an infection, prompting the body to generate antibodies against a specific pathogen. Later, if the vaccinated individual is exposed to that pathogen, the immune system recognizes it quickly and mounts a defense, preventing illness. Pennsylvania Measles Outbreak Surges to 424 Cases as Two Deaths Put Lancaster County at the Center of the Crisis - NCHStats
新闻简报截至2026年8月27日,宾夕法尼亚州一起 麻疹 暴发 疫情已激增至424例确诊病例,其中94例为上周新增。报告了两例与 麻疹 相关的死亡病例,其中包括一名婴儿,该婴儿检测呈 麻疹 阳性,但尚未达到常规 MMR 疫苗接种 的接种年龄。宾夕法尼亚州卫生部将这两例死亡归类为 麻疹 相关,但并非病毒直接致死。暴发 已扩散至兰开斯特县以外的地区,包括西莫兰县和巴特勒县,引发对更广泛 传播 的担忧。麻疹 传染性极强,可在空气中保持感染性长达两小时,进一步增加传播风险。自4月以来,该州已启动 疫苗接种 运动,通过91个临时诊所接种超过4,100剂 MMR 疫苗。官员警告称,随着新学年开学,若 传播 以当前速度持续,疫情可能进一步恶化。暴发 的发展轨迹仍存在不确定性,取决于病例数量是下降还是进一步上升。
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至2026年8月27日,暴发 疫情已激增至424例病例,其中94例为上周新增。自4月以来,该疫情迅速蔓延,近期已发布多项暴露预警,并对与学校相关的传播风险表示担忧。
地点与对象
暴发 疫情中心位于宾夕法尼亚州兰开斯特县,并已扩散至西莫兰县、巴特勒县等其他地区。受影响人群主要为未接种疫苗者,特别是兰开斯特县,该县幼儿园学生的 MMR 疫苗覆盖率已降至87.6%。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州正经历一起 麻疹 暴发 疫情,截至2026年8月27日确诊病例达424例,并报告两例 麻疹 相关死亡病例。其中一例死亡涉及一名婴儿,该婴儿检测呈 麻疹 阳性,但尚未达到常规 MMR 疫苗接种 的接种年龄。宾夕法尼亚州卫生部将这两例死亡归类为 麻疹 相关,但并非病毒直接致死。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 疫情规模显著扩大,上周新增94例病例,并已扩散至多个县。由于 麻疹 传染性极强且可在空气中保持感染性长达两小时,进一步传播的风险真实存在。自4月以来,该州已启动 疫苗接种 运动,包括设立91个临时诊所,累计接种超过4,100剂 MMR 疫苗。
应对、状态与不确定性
宾夕法尼亚州官方正积极应对 暴发 疫情,采取 疫苗接种 措施、隔离感染者及开展密切接触者追踪。该州已发布多项暴露预警,并密切监测疫情形势。暴发 的发展轨迹仍存在不确定性,取决于病例数量是继续上升还是下降。

Pennsylvania Measles Outbreak Surges to 424 Cases as Two Deaths Put Lancaster County at the Center of the Crisis - NCHStats

News Brief
A measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has surged to 424 confirmed cases as of August 27, 2026, with 94 cases diagnosed in the previous week. Two measles-associated deaths have been reported, including one involving an infant who had tested positive for the virus but was not old enough for routine MMR vaccination. The Pennsylvania Department of Health has classified these deaths as measles-associated, not necessarily due to the virus being the direct cause. The outbreak has spread to counties beyond Lancaster, including Westmoreland and Butler, raising concerns about wider transmission. Measles is highly contagious and can remain infectious in the air for up to two hours, increasing the risk of further spread. The state has launched vaccination campaigns, administering over 4,100 MMR doses through 91 pop-up clinics since April. Officials warn that the outbreak could escalate if transmission continues at the current pace, especially with the start of the school year. The outbreak's trajectory remains uncertain, depending on whether case numbers decline or rise further.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content North American Community Hub Statistics Home US Population News Blog Demographics Disease Data Hospital Data Birth and Fertility Mortality U.S Maps Cities Population States More About Us Editorial Policy Contact Us Home US Population News Blog Demographics Disease Data Hospital Data Birth and Fertility Mortality U.S Maps Cities Population States More About Us Editorial Policy Contact Us Search Discover more Vaccines & Immunizations infections City & Local Guides Pennsylvania Measles Outbreak Surges to 424 Cases as Two Deaths Put Lancaster County at the Center of the Crisis Carter Anderson Published: August 27, 2026 Updated: August 27, 2026 Disease Data, News Discover more Healthy Geographic Reference vaccine Pennsylvania is dealing with one of the fastest growing measles outbreaks in the country, and the latest numbers show that the problem is moving well beyond a small cluster in Lancaster County. By Wednesday evening, the Pennsylvania Department of Health had confirmed 424 measles cases in 2026. 94 of those cases were diagnosed in the previous week alone. New infections have also appeared in counties that had not reported cases earlier in the outbreak, including Westmoreland and Butler counties. The surge comes days after state officials confirmed two measles-associated deaths involving unvaccinated Lancaster County residents. They are the first deaths associated with measles in Pennsylvania in 35 years and the first reported in the United States this year. Table of Contents Toggle One of The Deaths Involved an Infant The Outbreak Is Growing Much Faster than It Was Earlier This Summer There Is a Real Risk of Wider Spread Lancaster County Has a Major Vaccination Gap The Next Few Weeks Will Show Whether Pennsylvania Can Contain It One of The Deaths Involved an Infant Discover more Biological Sciences Vaccine Health Conditions State health officials confirmed on Wednesday that one of the two people who died was an infant. The case has drawn additional scrutiny because records from the Lancaster County Coroner do not describe measles as the immediate cause of death. The coroner reported that the infant died from a lacerated spleen. The child had also tested positive for measles. The Pennsylvania Department of Health continues to classify the case as a measles-associated death. It means measles was linked to the case through laboratory or epidemiological evidence, but it does not necessarily mean the virus was listed as the direct medical cause of death. The state has released little information about the second death because of privacy concerns. Another point is important when discussing the infant. Routine MMR vaccination does not begin at birth, so the child would not have been old enough to receive the first standard dose. The Outbreak Is Growing Much Faster than It Was Earlier This Summer On August 25, the state reported 393 cases across 28 counties. By 6 p.m. on August 26, the total had reached 424. Local reporting also confirmed the first Westmoreland County case and a new case in Butler County. The increase follows an already sharp acceleration. The American Academy of Pediatrics reported last week that Pennsylvania had added 91 cases in a single week, helping drive a national increase of 211 cases. Lancaster County remains the main center of transmission, but the appearance of cases in additional counties shows why health officials are treating the situation as a statewide problem rather than a local outbreak. There Is a Real Risk of Wider Spread A much larger outbreak is possible if transmission continues at the current pace. Measles spreads through the air and is one of the most contagious human infections. The virus can remain infectious in an indoor space for up to two hours after an infected person has left. Symptoms can appear 7 to 21 days after exposure, according to Pennsylvania health guidance on measles, which means the official case count always reflects infections that began days or weeks earlier. Recent exposure alerts show how easily the virus can reach people outside the household. Pennsylvania officials warned this month about possible exposure at a soccer camp at Millersville University and at a Whole Foods Market in Lancaster. Earlier alerts involved a courthouse and other public locations. The start of the school year adds another concern. Schools create long periods of indoor contact, and some communities in Pennsylvania have vaccination rates below the level public health experts consider sufficient to prevent sustained measles transmission. The outbreak also comes shortly after a federal vaccine executive order targeting parts of the childhood immunization schedule, including future changes involving MMR vaccination. Lancaster County Has a Major Vaccination Gap Vaccination rates in Lancaster County have fallen substantially over the past decade. Recent reporting based on state data puts MMR coverage among kindergarten students in the county at about 87.6%, down from 95.5% in 2017. That gap is important because two doses of MMR provide about 97% protection against measles. High community coverage also limits the number of susceptible people the virus can reach. Pennsylvania has responded by dramatically increasing vaccination efforts. Since the outbreak began in April, state teams have administered more than 4,100 MMR doses through 91 pop-up clinics. Another 40 clinics are planned. More than 35,000 Pennsylvanians received an MMR vaccine in July, about 10,000 more than during a typical month, according to the state update on the expanded measles response. The Next Few Weeks Will Show Whether Pennsylvania Can Contain It If Pennsylvania continues adding close to 100 cases a week, the state could move rapidly toward several hundred additional infections before autumn. That outcome is not guaranteed. Vaccination campaigns, isolation of infected people and fast contact tracing can break transmission chains. The problem is that measles moves quickly through groups with low immunity. New cases in additional counties show that Lancaster County is no longer the only place that needs attention. For now, the most important number is 94. That is how many cases Pennsylvania added in one week. If that number begins falling, the outbreak may be coming under control. If it keeps rising as schools reopen, Pennsylvania could be entering a much larger phase of the outbreak. Related Posts: Two Georgia Residents Monitored After Cruise Ship... Location and population
The outbreak is centered in Lancaster County, Pennsylvania, and has spread to other counties such as Westmoreland and Butler. The affected population includes unvaccinated individuals, particularly in Lancaster County, where MMR coverage among kindergarten students has dropped to 87.6%.
Event

宾夕法尼亚州报告两例 麻疹 相关死亡,为美国 2026 年首例

Two dead in Pennsylvania measles outbreak, first in US in 2026
新闻简报2026 年 8 月,宾夕法尼亚州报告两例与 麻疹 相关的死亡病例,为该州当年首次出现此类病例。两名死者均未接种疫苗。此次 暴发 涉及 393 例确诊病例,发生在兰开斯特县。州卫生部门强调 麻疹 的严重性,指出该病毒已基本被消除超过 30 年。他们呼吁加强 疫苗接种,并强调 MMR 疫苗(麻疹-腮腺炎-风疹疫苗)安全有效,可提供 97% 的终身保护。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)报告称,美国 麻疹 病例已达 30 年来最高水平,其中 93% 的感染与未接种疫苗的个体相关。此次 暴发 突显疫苗犹豫症的风险,并强调维持高免疫覆盖率以预防严重并发症和死亡的重要性。

此次宾夕法尼亚州 麻疹 暴发报告于 2026 年 8 月 25 日,死亡病例发生在 2026 年。

此次 暴发 发生在宾夕法尼亚州,具体位于兰开斯特县,影响未接种疫苗的个体。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部门确认至少两人死于 麻疹。这是美国首次报告与 麻疹 相关的死亡病例。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年宾夕法尼亚州共报告 393 例 麻疹 确诊病例。死亡病例与未接种疫苗的个体相关,麻疹 相关死亡率为每 1,000 例病例中 1 至 3 例。CDC 指出,自该病毒基本被消除近 30 年来,美国 麻疹 病例已达历史最高水平。
应对、状态与不确定性
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部门确认了死亡病例并报告了此次 暴发。他们强调加强疫苗接种的重要性,并表示 MMR 疫苗安全有效。CDC 也提供了此次 暴发 的相关数据及其影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Two dead in Pennsylvania measles outbreak, first in US in 2026

News Brief
In August 2026, Pennsylvania reported two measles-related deaths, marking the first such cases in the U.S. that year. Both individuals were unvaccinated, and the outbreak, which included 393 confirmed cases, occurred in Lancaster County. The state health department highlighted the severity of measles, noting that it has been largely eliminated for over three decades. They urged vaccination, emphasizing that the MMR vaccine is safe and provides 97% lifetime protection. The CDC reported that measles cases have reached a 30-year high in the U.S., with 93% of infections linked to unvaccinated individuals. The outbreak underscores the risks of vaccine hesitancy and the importance of maintaining immunization rates to prevent severe complications and deaths.
Original source text (English)
News Sports Things To Do Opinion Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals HEALTH Two dead in Pennsylvania measles outbreak, first in US in 2026 Saman Shafiq USA TODAY Updated Aug. 25, 2026, 1:04 p.m. ET At least two people have died of measles in Pennsylvania, the state’s health department said, adding that both were unvaccinated. The measles deaths were the first in Pennsylvania in 35 years, the state Department of Health said in a news release Aug. 25, and the first such deaths in the United States this year, according to Centers for Disease Control and Prevention data. “Because measles was largely eliminated in the Commonwealth for more than three decades, people are not familiar with this disease and don’t fully understand the potential severity of the illness,” Pennsylvania Secretary of Health Dr. Debra Bogen said in a statement. “As a physician, I want to make sure that people understand that the MMR vaccine is safe and provides the best protection we have against measles.” Both patients lived in Lancaster County, the health department said, adding that it would not identify them to protect their and their families' privacy. Authorities have confirmed 393 cases of measles in Pennsylvania in 2026, according to the news release. What is measles, and how do you get it? Measles is a highly contagious virus that can cause severe short- and long-term health problems, including pneumonia and brain swelling. Nearly 20% of people who contract measles end up in the hospital. Though measles-related deaths are rare, they are not uncommon, occurring in 1 to 3 patients per 1,000 cases, according to Pennsylvania health authorities. "The best protection against measles is getting fully vaccinated," the state health department said. "The MMR vaccine, administered over two doses, boosts a person’s immune system and provides 97% lifetime protection." The measles, mumps and rubella vaccine is usually given in two doses around ages 1 to 5. The CDC also notes that adults and teens should also be up to date on MMR vaccinations. Is the MMR vaccine safe? Medical experts have long said the MMR vaccine is safe and effective. For decades, the vaccine has been administered to millions of children, helping prevent severe and life-threatening complications of measles, mumps and rubella. Serious side effects of the vaccine, including seizures, are rare. The vaccine has been available in its current form since 1971, marking more than 50 years of safe and effective use. It also has been instrumental in curtailing the spread of measles, which, before the vaccine's introduction in 1963, sickened an estimated 3 million to 4 million people in the United States every year and causing 500 deaths and 48,000 hospitalizations. Measles cases reach record high Measles cases in the United States have surpassed last year's total and are at their highest since the disease was eliminated from the country nearly three decades ago, according to CDC data. The majority, 93%, occurred in unvaccinated people, and nearly 69% of cases were in people ages 19 and younger. Cases reached a 30-year high in 2025, with 2,289 confirmed infections across 43 states and the District of Columbia. Nearly 90% of cases were directly linked to 48 known outbreaks, and 93% of infections were contracted by unvaccinated people or those whose vaccination status was unknown. Three people, including two children, died as a result of the outbreaks, the first pediatric measles deaths in a decade. Dr. Mehmet Oz contradicts Trump's claims on vaccine President Donald Trump, meanwhile, signed an executive order Aug. 10 calling for more vaccine “flexibility,” fewer childhood vaccinations and splitting the combined childhood measles, mumps and rubella vaccine into three shots at different times. The order also says all childhood vaccines should be spaced out, with each one administered in a separate doctor's visit "to the maximum extent feasible." Announcing the order, Trump said the MMR vaccine could be “quite lethal.” Experts, however, say the vaccine is safe and effective, and research indicates dividing it into multiple shots has no health benefit. Dr. Mehmet Oz, head of the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services, contradicted Trump’s statement about the MMR shot in an interview Aug. 23 on CBS’ "Face the Nation," saying it is “not a lethal vaccine.” Contributing: Zac Anderson and Mary Walrath-Holdridge Saman Shafiq is a trending news reporter for USA TODAY. Reach her at sshafiq@usatodayco.com and follow her on X and Instagram @saman_shafiq7. Featured Weekly Ad

Scale, impact and risk
There were 393 confirmed measles cases in Pennsylvania in 2026. The deaths were linked to unvaccinated individuals, and measles-related deaths occur in 1 to 3 patients per 1,000 cases. The CDC notes that measles cases have reached record highs in the U.S. since its elimination nearly three decades ago.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Pennsylvania Department of Health confirmed the deaths and reported the outbreak. They emphasized the importance of vaccination and stated that the MMR vaccine is safe and effective. The CDC also provided data on the outbreak and its implications.

DPH Confirms Four Additional Measles Cases in Georgia - Georgia Coastal Health District

News Brief
The Georgia Department of Public Health (DPH) confirmed four additional measles cases in the metro Atlanta area on August 28, 2026. This follows the confirmation of a measles case in an unvaccinated resident of metro Atlanta on August 20, 2026. The DPH reiterated that the MMR vaccine is safe and effective in preventing measles. The cases were reported within the Coastal Health District of Georgia, which serves Glynn County and other regions. No further details on investigation, testing, or containment measures were provided. The measles virus, historically known as rubeola, is the focus of this report, with no related entities or hard exclusions mentioned.

宾夕法尼亚州麻疹死亡事件凸显疫苗报告的必要性 - 健康新闻记者协会

Pennsylvania measles deaths highlight need for vaccine reporting - Association of Health Care Journalists
新闻简报《健康新闻记者协会》(AHCJ)于2026年8月25日发布的文章《宾夕法尼亚州麻疹死亡事件凸显疫苗报告的必要性》探讨了麻疹死亡事件在宾夕法尼亚州的影响,并强调了准确疫苗报告的重要性。该文章未提供具体的病例或死亡人数,但指出因疫苗犹豫及公共卫生沟通不足导致的麻疹 暴发风险。文章呼吁记者与卫生官员提升透明度与对疫苗接种工作的认知。文章未详述任何具体调查或应对措施,而是聚焦于公共卫生报告实践不当的更广泛影响。该内容属于AHCJ为提升健康新闻报道、确保重大健康问题得到应有关注而开展的更广泛工作的一部分。

Pennsylvania measles deaths highlight need for vaccine reporting - Association of Health Care Journalists

News Brief
The article 'Pennsylvania measles deaths highlight need for vaccine reporting' by the Association of Health Care Journalists (AHCJ) published on August 25, 2026, discusses the impact of measles deaths in Pennsylvania and underscores the importance of accurate vaccine reporting. While the piece does not provide specific case or death counts, it highlights the risk of measles outbreaks due to vaccine hesitancy and inadequate public health communication. The article serves as a call to action for journalists and health officials to improve transparency and awareness around vaccination efforts. It does not detail any specific investigation or response measures, focusing instead on the broader implications of poor reporting practices in public health. The content is part of AHCJ's broader efforts to enhance health journalism and ensure that critical health issues receive proper attention.

2 unvaccinated Pennsylvanians die after contracting measles as outbreak reaches nearly 400 cases - WHYY

News Brief
Two unvaccinated individuals in Pennsylvania died after contracting measles, as an outbreak reached nearly 400 cases. The report, issued by WHYY on August 25, 2026, highlights the severity of the outbreak and the impact of unvaccinated populations. The outbreak is under investigation, and the source status is full, meaning the original article body was extracted. The event underscores the importance of vaccination in preventing measles transmission and its associated risks. While the report does not explicitly state the risk level, the high number of cases and deaths indicates a significant public health concern. The outbreak serves as a reminder of the potential consequences of vaccine hesitancy and the need for continued efforts in immunization programs.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

疫苗 谣言四起:RFK Jr 称两例 麻疹 死亡系“捏造” - 《卫报》

Vaccine misinformation spreads as RFK Jr suggests two measles deaths were ‘fabricated’ - The Guardian
新闻简报《卫报》于 2026-08-28 报道称,美国卫生部长小罗伯特·F·肯尼迪(Robert F Kennedy Jr)暗示,宾夕法尼亚州两例与 麻疹 相关的死亡可能系“捏造”,此言论引发了关于 疫苗 谣言的争议。这些死亡病例已被州卫生部门及美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)确认,涉及未接种疫苗的人群,包括一名新生儿。反 疫苗 活动人士(包括与肯尼迪有关联者)正试图联系死者家属并散布阴谋论。CDC 正在调查这些死亡是否由 麻疹 导致,而州卫生部门则强调已对病例进行了全面审查。暴发 事件引发了对谣言对 疫苗接种 接种率及公共卫生影响的担忧。所有患病个体均未完成全程疫苗接种,而 暴发 事件也使外界对肯尼迪在推动反 疫苗 言论中的角色展开了更严格的审视。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发布于 2026-08-28。事件日期未明确说明,但宾夕法尼亚州的 麻疹 相关死亡病例报告于 2026 年 6 月下旬,CDC 截至 2026-08-26 周四仍在努力确定其病因。
地点与对象
麻疹 相关死亡病例发生于宾夕法尼亚州兰开斯特县,该地区有大量阿米什(Amish)和门诺(Mennonite)社区。受影响人群均为未接种疫苗者,除未接种疫苗外,未提及其他具体人群特征。
事件
宾夕法尼亚州报告了两例 麻疹 相关死亡病例,其中包括一名新生儿。这些死亡病例已被该州卫生部门及 CDC 团队确认。美国卫生部长小罗伯特·F·肯尼迪暗示这些死亡可能系“捏造”,并质疑 CDC 的介入,而反 疫苗 活动人士则散布关于这些死亡的阴谋论。
规模、影响与风险
约 424 名宾夕法尼亚州居民确诊 麻疹 感染,其中 73 人住院。所有患病个体均未完成全程疫苗接种。CDC 正在调查这些死亡是否由 麻疹 导致。暴发 事件引发了对 疫苗接种 效果及谣言传播的担忧,后者可能导致更多病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在努力确定这些死亡的实际原因及其是否由 麻疹 导致。州卫生部门已确认这些死亡并表示已进行全面调查。反 疫苗 活动人士试图联系死者家属并散布虚假信息。美国卫生与公众服务部(HHS)未回应《卫报》关于肯尼迪对 疫苗接种 立场的提问。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaccine misinformation spreads as RFK Jr suggests two measles deaths were ‘fabricated’ - The Guardian

News Brief
The Guardian reported on 2026-08-28 that Robert F Kennedy Jr, the US health secretary, suggested two measles-associated deaths in Pennsylvania may have been 'fabricated,' sparking a debate over vaccine misinformation. The deaths, confirmed by the state health department and CDC, involved unvaccinated individuals, including a newborn. Anti-vaccine activists, including those linked to Kennedy, are attempting to track down the families of the deceased and spread conspiracy theories. The CDC is investigating whether the deaths were caused by measles, while the state health department emphasized thorough case reviews. The outbreak has raised concerns about the impact of misinformation on vaccination rates and public health. None of the sickened individuals are fully vaccinated, and the outbreak has led to increased scrutiny of Kennedy’s role in promoting anti-vaccine sentiments.
Original source text (English)
